Every fixed (crossed-out) item from `FEEDBACK.md`, moved here unchanged on Nov 16, 2026 so the open list stays short.
Nothing was deleted. Newest fixes go at the top of their group. If a fixed thing comes back, give it 🔁 and add it to
"Things that keep coming back" in `FEEDBACK.md`.

Charging: head down, back end up, red eyes, and the cracks in the shell glow like coals. Standing still, all six feet are on the ground; walking uses a real insect walk (two sets of three legs in turns). The Great one has spike rows, bigger spikes and side horns. *From: you. Said: 1×*

The host keeps each statue (where, its loot, hits, time left); every computer turns its own fallen body to stone (a crack-and-stone layer drawn over its paint) and it becomes solid like a boulder. Each pickaxe hit deepens the cracks; the last bursts it into chunks and drops its stone (one per hit) and loot. Bosses still hand out their gifts at once. Now each page rock touches the floor with its exact shape and other rocks with balls fitted inside it; measured in a test: deepest dip into the floor 0.2 cm, none floating, none falling through, ~2 ms per step; they shrink away after 4.5 s like the game's. Your next note: the wait before the stone too long; the page rocks huge and shrinking too fast. → The stone starts sooner (wait at most 1.8 s; wings and eyes settle again if it still rolls; it only freezes once still); a page bug made rocks up to 8x too big before they shrank (fixed); each chunk now shrinks at its own time (4-6 s) over 1.5 s, in the game too. Your next notes: the heavy back is done, but the very end has no bone (it hooks into his body) and behind the hair his neck looks stretched out. → A bone added at the very ends of the hair; the ponytail's collision size matches the hair's thickness; the hair right against the neck now stays with the head. Your next notes (Nov 17, with pictures): it lifts off his back like a stiff slab (a "visor"), the bare neck shows under it, the tip hooks. → Nov 17 (evening), measured instead of guessed (new numbers in the clip check: how far the back hair lifts off the back and opens away from it). Five real causes: (1) the chain's last two bones folded back up into his neck (the nape hair rode on its swinging end: the bare neck and the hook); (2) its drag worked against the world, so running acted like a headwind and blew it out flat; (3) the collision balls (each riding one bone, the arm ones fat) poked out of the bending shirt and pushed it off; (4) where the chain bends, the hair beside it followed two bones on either side of a line and tore into a sheet; (5) the hanging part had almost no locks (a bare smooth sheet). Fixed: the chain runs straight down the middle to the lowest hair; below the head the hair lies on the shirt and moves with it (no headwind, no balls: it may lift off a little, never sink in); the hair is weighed to its bones by height (no tear line); the bone over the neck stretches like skin; four rows of hanging locks cover the hanging part. Worst lift off the back: run 11.5 → 2.9 cm, mining 17.4 → 2.8, sword 11.0 → 2.3, roll 21.8 → 6.9; worst tear on the back 8x → 4x; the back hair no longer goes into the neck at all. 3D page "Dwarf Body" updated. - [x] ~~**N2. The front braids have the same "headwind" the back hair had.**~~ Their swing slows them against the world, so when he runs they trail behind as if in a strong wind, and the fat collision balls round the upper arms push them about. Found by Claude on the PC, Nov 17. Fix when you say yes. Crossed off Nov 19 (owner review): good for now, 8/10; improve later.

- [x] ~~**F702. 🔁 Hold Ctrl to dash farther: a short tap gives a short dash, holding it longer should give a longer dash.**~~ 1st time (Nov 22): held dash keeps going up to 0.75 s instead of cutting off early. 2nd time (Nov 22 night): "If I press Ctrl and hold, I dodge exactly the same distance, and then I basically sprint where I should have landed in the first place" (that round fixed a different bug in the same report, the no-movement-key dodge direction flip-flop, not this one). 3rd time (Nov 23, PC): "I think I realized the problem. In order for me to do a long dodge, I am holding the control key all the way until the dwarf stops rolling... after my animation of the dodge is done, he propels me forward." *From: you (PC). Said: 4×* **Done Nov 23 (3rd try, different approach):** the real dash distance was already growing the longer you held — only the roll ANIMATION couldn't keep up. It was always stretched to finish in the same 0.75 s a held dash is capped at, same as a quick tap; a tap stops moving well before 0.75 s so the stand-up pose plays while he's already still (fine), but a held dash kept speeding up (a 1.8x multiplier, added in the 1st fix) right up to that same 0.75 s, so the stand-up played out mid-burst — looking exactly like the animation ending and physics shoving him the rest of the way. Checked with a robot (`src/debug/dashcheck.gd`) + screenshots: before the fix he's fully standing at t=0.75 s while still at top dash speed; gave the clip a 0.3 s recovery tail so it finishes after the burst instead, during the natural slow-down — the same robot now shows him still crouched/rolling at t=0.75 s. New test `test_held_dash_roll_still_has_recovery_left_when_the_dash_ends`; `test_roll_animation_matches_how_long_the_dash_lasts` updated to match. `src/combat/combat.gd` (new `DASH_RECOVERY_TAIL` constant), `src/combat/dwarf_fight.gd` (`dodge_expected_duration()`). 4th time (Nov 23, PC, after playing the above fix): "the animation is slower now... but we still have the same problem where he speeds up at the end of the dodge... the entire speed at the beginning and the end should be exactly the same." **Root cause, finally:** that 1.8x speed multiplier from the 1st fix was never actually removed — it made holding the dash really travel farther by making him go FASTER near the end, not just longer. That's exactly the "speeding up" the 3rd fix had worked around rather than removed. **Done Nov 23 (4th try, real fix):** deleted the multiplier entirely. A dash now moves at the same constant speed start to finish, tap or held; holding only makes it last longer (toward the 0.75 s cap), never faster. Checked with the same robot: `vel` holds flat at 6.67 the whole dash (was ramping to ~11.7 by the end), only slowing down naturally after the dash ends. New test `test_held_dash_moves_at_the_same_speed_start_to_finish`.

So the game showed the first-version bricks (old limestone, darker). Fixed by touching the 3 files and re-importing. - [x] ~~**F861. A big gap where two rows of a steep wood roof meet**~~ (Smeltery hall roof, wood version: a dark slit along the whole roof halfway up). Every wood building with two rows of steep roof has it. Fix in the model (shingles reach the box edges, MODEL-RULES checks), not the blueprint. The brick one has a small 6 cm version. *From: you (PC, Nov 25, Smeltery page; Nov 25 in the game, picture from inside). Said: 2×* **Done Nov 25:** the real cause: the wood roof models were built at a gentler angle than the roof line (a steep piece climbed 1.5 m instead of 2 m), so each row ended ~40 cm below where the next began. The 6 Shack sloped roofs (steep, gentle, inner and outer corners; `shack_v2.py` roof_v3/roof_corner_v3) now climb the full rise, the shingles run up under the top log, and a hidden dark board under the high edge and one side closes the 1 cm cracks between rows and between neighbours. Brick roofs (6 cm version) left alone: another chat has open work there.
